### Tool result

_source line 109 · 2026-05-15T01:08:58.777Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_018zRJceHQXRywWwEn67p1v6`</summary>

```text
appendix.tex:109:The dose-response pattern ($\beta{=}0.1$: proxy gaming inflates ISR without genuine improvement; $\beta{=}0.3$: PR collapse to 18\% vs.\ 48\% random) maps onto the trajectory predicted by~\cite{gao-reward-overoptimization}, but at a meta level: the proxy being gamed is itself a gaming detector.
conclusion.tex:15:The dose-response pattern ($\beta{=}0.1$: proxy gaming; $\beta{=}0.3$: collapse) maps onto~\cite{gao-reward-overoptimization} at a meta level.
experiments.tex:8:We train Qwen3-4B with GRPO~\citep{deepseekmath} ($K{=}8$ rollouts, 500 steps default, 1000 where noted) across seeds $\{42,43,44\}$ on a fixed 500-example FOLIO~\citep{folio} subset held constant across conditions. %% data: C001
experiments.tex:39:ISR-as-reward provides the first empirical demonstration of Goodhart's Law at the meta level~\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}: the diagnostic itself becomes the target of gaming when optimized.
experiments.tex:75:At $\beta{=}0.3$, ISR causes PR collapse to 18\% (vs.\ 48\% random), completing the Goodhart trajectory: low-dose gaming ($\beta{=}0.1$) gives way to high-dose collapse~\cite{gao-reward-overoptimization}. %% registry: plan002-additive-b03-s42, plan017-additive-random-s42
introduction.tex:8:Prove rate---the dominant evaluation metric for neurosymbolic formalizers---conflates faithful reasoning with degenerate shortcuts, a gap especially consequential under reinforcement learning, where the optimizer exploits any shortcut the reward fails to penalize~\cite{skalse-reward-hacking, reward-hacking-survey}.
introduction.tex:9:Recent work applies GRPO~\cite{deepseekmath} to train LLMs that generate solver-verified formal programs~\cite{mellgren-prolog-grpo, logic-rl} using binary execution feedback as reward; this drives rapid PR gains but gives no gradient on structural quality.
introduction.tex:10:Process reward models~\cite{lightman-verify-step} offer step-level supervision but require expensive annotations and do not transfer to arbitrary solver-backed settings.
introduction.tex:13:We address this gap with \emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), a diagnostic protocol inspired by mutation testing~\cite{demillo1978} that applies component necessity testing---leave-one-out (LOO) ablation on solver inputs---to quantify structural integrity (\autoref{fig:sta-overview}).
introduction.tex:17:Applied as a post-hoc diagnostic on a GRPO formalizer trained with FOLIO~\cite{folio} data, ISR reveals a structural collapse trajectory entirely invisible to prove rate: ISR falls from 0.642 at 100 steps to 0.132 at 500 steps to 0.003 at 1000 steps---a 99.5\% degradation---while prove rate simultaneously rises from 32\% to 96\%.
introduction.tex:21:A natural question is whether ISR can serve as a training signal. The answer is negative: when ISR is added to the reward ($r = r_{\text{exec}} \cdot (1 + \beta \cdot \text{ISR})$), the model games the diagnostic itself---\emph{meta-level Goodhart's Law}~\cite{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy, karwowski-goodhart-rl}.
limitations.tex:10:Our experiments use 500 training examples and 100 evaluation examples, reflecting the FOLIO dataset's total size of 1,435 annotated examples~\citep{folio}.
method.tex:15:We train with GRPO~\citep{deepseekmath} ($K{=}8$ rollouts), where each rollout receives binary execution reward $r_{\text{exec}}(x) = \mathbf{1}[S(F_x) = \textit{proved}]$.
related_work.tex:5:Prompting-based pipelines couple LLMs with external solvers---SatLM~\cite{satlm}, Logic-LM~\cite{logic-lm}, LINC~\cite{linc}, LoRP~\cite{lorp}, FOVER~\cite{fover-pei2025}---but evaluate execution accuracy alone without diagnosing structural quality.
related_work.tex:6:RL approaches train against solver feedback~\cite{mellgren-prolog-grpo, logic-rl, thought-like-pro}, optimizing prove rate with binary reward.
related_work.tex:8:We use FOLIO~\cite{folio}, the only public NLI benchmark with both FOL and Prolog annotations enabling cross-logic diagnostic transfer.
related_work.tex:11:Learned PRMs~\cite{lightman-verify-step, math-shepherd} provide step-level feedback but suffer from design-choice sensitivity~\cite{gao-effective-rl-reward} and distribution shift~\cite{processbench}; recent advances~\cite{thinkprm, versaprm, rprm} improve quality but remain learned and require curated supervision.
related_work.tex:16:Goodhart's Law is established theoretically~\cite{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy, skalse-reward-hacking, karwowski-goodhart-rl} and empirically in RLHF~\cite{gao-reward-overoptimization} and frontier models~\cite{metr-reward-hacking}; specification gaming~\cite{bondarenko-spec-gaming}, formalization gaming~\cite{kim-game-formalization}, and spurious rewards~\cite{spurious-rewards} have been documented, but the proxy is a learned reward model.
related_work.tex:17:Kim et al.~\cite{kim-game-formalization} diagnose gaming in inference-time outputs of frontier models; we study gaming that \emph{emerges during RL training} with a systematic taxonomy and cross-logic analysis.
related_work.tex:21:STA adapts the LOO mutation paradigm~\cite{demillo1978, jia-harman-survey} from software testing to solver inputs.
```

</details>

/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_3/projects/solver-trace-ablation/paper/abstract.tex:2:GRPO-trained neurosymbolic formalizers achieve high prove rates---93\% on FOLIO---yet 87\% of proved outputs embed the conclusion verbatim among the premises, a degenerate shortcut invisible to execution-based evaluation. We introduce \emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), a diagnostic (solver-agnostic in principle, validated on two structurally different solvers: Prover9 and SWI-Prolog) that applies leave-one-out component necessity testing to solver inputs. The fraction of components whose removal causes proof failure yields the \emph{Intensional Structural Reward} (ISR): ISR$=$1 indicates syntactic component necessity; ISR$=$0 indicates complete redundancy. ISR does not correlate with downstream NLI accuracy ($\rho{=}{-}0.049$, $p{=}0.187$), confirming it measures structural properties orthogonal to semantic correctness.
